<p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>

Kingdom</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data">All <a href="/wiki/Christian_denomination" title="Christian denomination">Christian denominations</a> that venerate <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saints</a>, especially <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">Canonized</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Pre-Congregation" <a href="/wiki/Mylapore" title="Mylapore">Mylapore</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chennai" title="Chennai">Chennai</a>, India
<br /><a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_St._Thomas_the_Apostle,_Ortona" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of St. Thomas the Apostle, Ortona">Basilica of St. Thomas the Apostle</a> in <a href="/wiki/Ortona" title="Ortona">Ortona</a>, Italy</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">Feast</a></th><td class="infobox-data">

<li>21 December: Some <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Communion" title="Anglican Communion">Anglican Communion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mozarabic_rite" class="mw-redirect" title="Mozarabic rite">Hispanic church</a>, <a href="/wiki/Traditionalist_Catholicism" title="Traditionalist Catholicism">Traditional Catholics</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lutherans" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutherans">Lutherans</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pashons_26_(Coptic_Orthodox_liturgics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pashons 26 (Coptic Orthodox liturgics)">26 Pashons</a> and Sunday after Easter (<a href="/wiki/Thomas_Sunday" class="mw-redirect" title="Thomas Sunday">Thomas Sunday</a>): <a href="/wiki/Coptic_Orthodox_Church_of_Alexandria" class="mw-redirect" title="Coptic Orthodox Church of Alexandria">Coptic Christianity</a><sup id="cite_ref-st-takla.org_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-st-takla.org-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>6 October and Sunday after Easter <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Sunday" class="mw-redirect" title="Thomas Sunday">Thomas Sunday</a>: <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox</a></li></ul>
</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data">The Twin, placing his finger in the side of Christ, <a href="/wiki/Nelumbo_nucifera" title="Nelumbo nucifera">nelumbo nucifera</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spear" title="Spear">spear</a> (means of his <a href="/wiki/Christian_martyrdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Christian martyrdom">Christian martyrdom</a>), <a href="/wiki/Carpentry_square" class="mw-redirect" title="Carpentry square">square</a> (his profession, a builder)</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Architects" class="mw-redirect" title="Architects">Architects</a>, for Christians in India (including <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a> and <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Madras_and_Mylapore" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Madras and Mylapore">Archdiocese of Madras-Mylapore</a>), <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka" title="Sri Lanka">Sri Lanka</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pula" title="Pula">Pula (Croatia)</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Thomas the Apostle</b> (<a href="/wiki/Greek_language" title="Greek language">Greek</a>: <span lang="el">Θωμᾶς</span>, <a href="/wiki/Syriac_alphabet" title="Syriac alphabet">Syriac</a> ܬܐܘܡܐ, <span title="Imperial Aramaic (700-300 BCE)-language romanization"><i lang="arc-Latn"><i>Tʾōmā</i></i></span>, meaning "the twin"),<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> also known as <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text"><i lang="grc"><b>Didymus</b></i></span> (Greek: Δίδυμος <i>Didymos,</i> meaning "twin"), was one of the <a href="/wiki/Twelve_Apostles" class="mw-redirect" title="Twelve Apostles">Twelve Apostles</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jesus" title="Jesus">Jesus</a> according to the <a href="/wiki/New_Testament" title="New Testament">New Testament</a>. Thomas is commonly known as "<a href="/wiki/Doubting_Thomas" title="Doubting Thomas">Doubting Thomas</a>" because he initially doubted the <a href="/wiki/Resurrection_of_Jesus_Christ" class="mw-redirect" title="Resurrection of Jesus Christ">resurrection of Jesus Christ</a> when he was told of it (as is related in the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_John" title="Gospel of John">Gospel of John</a>); he later confessed his faith ("My lord and my God") on seeing the <a href="/wiki/Five_Holy_Wounds" title="Five Holy Wounds">wounds</a> left over from the <a href="/wiki/Crucifixion" title="Crucifixion">crucifixion</a>. He was Jewish.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Thomas_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena,_Italy.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Thomas_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/220px-Thomas_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="195" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Thomas_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/330px-Thomas_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Thomas_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg/440px-Thomas_the_Apostle._Detail_of_the_mosaic_in_the_Basilica_of_San_Vitale._Ravena%2C_Italy.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1016" data-file-height="900" /></a><figcaption>Thomas the Apostle, detail of the mosaic in the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_San_Vitale" title="Basilica of San Vitale">Basilica of San Vitale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ravenna" title="Ravenna">Ravenna</a>, 6th century</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to traditional accounts of the <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a> of modern-day states of <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kerala" title="Kerala">Kerala</a> in India, Saint Thomas travelled outside the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Empire" title="Roman Empire">Roman Empire</a> to preach the Gospel, travelling as far as the <a href="/wiki/Tamilakam" title="Tamilakam">Tamilakam</a> which is in South India, <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a>, <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFahlbuschBromileyLochmanMbiti2008285_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFahlbuschBromileyLochmanMbiti2008285-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESlapak199527_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESlapak199527-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMedlycott1905_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMedlycott1905-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPuthiakunnel1973_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPuthiakunnel1973-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> and reached <a href="/wiki/Muziris" title="Muziris">Muziris</a> of <a href="/wiki/Tamilakam" title="Tamilakam">Tamilakam</a> (modern-day North Paravur and Kodungalloor in <a href="/wiki/Kerala" title="Kerala">Kerala</a> State, India) in AD 52.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJohnsonZacharia2016_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJohnsonZacharia2016-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-stthoma_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stthoma-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFahlbuschBromileyLochmanMbiti2008285_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFahlbuschBromileyLochmanMbiti2008285-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> In 1258, some of the relics were brought to <a href="/wiki/Ortona" title="Ortona">Ortona</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Abruzzo" title="Abruzzo">Abruzzo</a>, Italy, where they have been held in the Church of Saint Thomas the Apostle.<sup id="cite_ref-gcatholic.org_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gcatholic.org-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> He is regarded as the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of India among its <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christian</a> adherents,<sup id="cite_ref-catholic-saints.info_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-saints.info-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMedlycott1905Ch._IV_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMedlycott1905Ch._IV-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> and the Feast of Saint Thomas on July 3 is celebrated as Indian Christians' Day.<sup id="cite_ref-Carvalho2021_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Carvalho2021-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Kumar2021_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kumar2021-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The name <i>Thomas</i> remains quite popular among the Saint Thomas Christians of the Indian subcontinent.
</p><p>Many churches in the Middle East and southern Asia, besides India, also mention Apostle Thomas in their historical traditions as being the first evangelist to establish those churches, the <a href="/wiki/Assyrian_Church_of_the_East" title="Assyrian Church of the East">Assyrian Church of the East</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> the early church of Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Gospel_of_John">Gospel of John</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Gospel of John">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Thomas first speaks in the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_John" title="Gospel of John">Gospel of John</a>. In John 11:16,<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> when <a href="/wiki/Lazarus_of_Bethany" title="Lazarus of Bethany">Lazarus</a> has recently died, and the apostles do not wish to go back to <a href="/wiki/Judea" title="Judea">Judea</a>, Thomas says: "Let us also go, that we may die with him."<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Thomas speaks again in John 14:5. There, <a href="/wiki/Jesus" title="Jesus">Jesus</a> had just explained that he was going away to prepare a heavenly home for his followers, and that one day they would join him there. Thomas reacted by saying, "Lord, we know not whither thou goest; and how can we know the way?"<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>John 20:24–29<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> tells how <a href="/wiki/Doubting_Thomas" title="Doubting Thomas">doubting Thomas</a> was skeptical at first when he heard that Jesus had risen from the dead and appeared to the other apostles, saying, "Except I shall see on his hands the print of the nails, and put my finger into the print of the nails, and thrust my hand into his side, I will not believe."<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> But when Jesus appeared later and invited Thomas to touch his wounds and behold him, Thomas showed his belief by saying, "My lord and my God".<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Jesus then said, "Thomas, because thou hast seen me, thou hast believed: blessed are they that have not seen, and yet have believed."<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Names_and_etymologies">Names and etymologies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Names and etymologies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The name <i>Thomas</i> (<a href="/wiki/Greek_language" title="Greek language">Greek</a>: Θωμᾶς) given for the apostle in the New Testament is derived from the <a href="/wiki/Aramaic" title="Aramaic">Aramaic</a> תְּאוֹמָא <i>Tʾōmā</i><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> (<a href="/wiki/Syriac_alphabet" title="Syriac alphabet">Syriac</a> ܬܐܘܿܡܵܐ/ܬ݁ܳܐܘܡܰܐ <i>Tʾōmā</i>/<i>Tāʾwma</i>), meaning "the twin" and cognate to <a href="/wiki/Hebrew_language" title="Hebrew language">Hebrew</a> תְּאוֹם <i>tʾóm</i>. The equivalent term for twin in Greek, which is also used in the New Testament, is Δίδυμος <i>Didymos</i>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_names">Other names</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Other names">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Nag_Hammadi_library" title="Nag Hammadi library">Nag Hammadi</a> copy of the <i><a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_Thomas" title="Gospel of Thomas">Gospel of Thomas</a></i> begins: "These are the secret sayings that the living Jesus spoke and Didymus, Judas Thomas, recorded." Early Syrian traditions also relate the apostle's full name as Judas Thomas.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> Some have seen in the <i><a href="/wiki/Acts_of_Thomas" title="Acts of Thomas">Acts of Thomas</a></i> (written in east Syria in the early 3rd century, or perhaps as early as the first half of the 2nd century) an identification of Thomas with the apostle <a href="/wiki/Jude_the_Apostle" title="Jude the Apostle">Judas, Son of James</a>. However, the first sentence of the Acts follows the Gospels and the Acts of the Apostles in distinguishing the apostle Thomas and the apostle Judas son of James. Others, such as <a href="/wiki/James_Tabor" title="James Tabor">James Tabor</a>, identify him as <a href="/wiki/Jude,_brother_of_Jesus" title="Jude, brother of Jesus">Jude, brother of Jesus</a> mentioned by Mark. In the <a href="/wiki/Book_of_Thomas_the_Contender" title="Book of Thomas the Contender">Book of Thomas the Contender</a>, part of the <a href="/wiki/Nag_Hammadi_library" title="Nag Hammadi library">Nag Hammadi library</a>, he is alleged to be a twin to Jesus: "Now, since it has been said that you are my twin and true companion, examine yourself…"<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThomas_the_Apostlen.d._29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas_the_Apostlen.d.-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A "<a href="/wiki/Doubting_Thomas" title="Doubting Thomas">Doubting Thomas</a>" is a skeptic who refuses to believe without direct personal experience—a reference to the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_John" title="Gospel of John">Gospel of John</a>'s depiction of the Apostle Thomas, who, in John's account, refused to believe the resurrected <a href="/wiki/Jesus" title="Jesus">Jesus</a> had appeared to the ten other apostles until he could see and feel Jesus' <a href="/wiki/Five_Holy_Wounds" title="Five Holy Wounds">crucifixion wounds</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Feast_days">Feast days</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Feast days">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>When the feast of Saint Thomas was inserted in the Roman calendar in the 9th century, it was assigned to 21 December. The <i>Martyrology of St. Jerome</i> mentioned the apostle on 3 July, the date to which the Roman celebration was transferred in 1969, so that it would no longer interfere with the major ferial days of <a href="/wiki/Advent" title="Advent">Advent</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECatholic_Church196996_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECatholic_Church196996-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Traditionalist Roman Catholics (who follow the <a href="/wiki/General_Roman_Calendar_of_1960" title="General Roman Calendar of 1960">General Roman Calendar of 1960</a> or earlier), the <a href="/wiki/Lutheran_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran Church">Lutheran Church</a>, and many <a href="/wiki/Anglicans" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglicans">Anglicans</a> (including members of the <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_Church_(United_States)" title="Episcopal Church (United States)">Episcopal Church</a> as well as members of the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> who worship according to the 1662 edition of the <a href="/wiki/Book_of_Common_Prayer" title="Book of Common Prayer">Book of Common Prayer</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-commonprayer.org_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-commonprayer.org-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> still celebrate his feast day on 21 December. However, most modern liturgical calendars (including the <a href="/wiki/Common_Worship" title="Common Worship">Common Worship</a> calendar of the Church of England) prefer 3 July, Thomas is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Festival_(Anglicanism)" title="Festival (Anglicanism)">Festival</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Eastern Orthodox venerates Thomas on the following days:
</p>
<ul><li>June 20 – Commemoration of the Translation of the Relics of the Apostles Andrew, Thomas, and Luke; the Prophet Elisha; and the Martyr Lazarus.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>June 30 – The Twelve Apostles.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>October 6 – Primary feast day.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The First Sunday after Easter – The Sunday of Thomas, which commemorate when Thomas' doubts regarding the risen Christ was removed by his touching of Christ's side.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>Thomas is also associated with the "Arabian" (or "Arapet") <a href="/wiki/Icon" title="Icon">icon</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Theotokos" title="Theotokos">Theotokos</a> (Mother of God), which is commemorated on 6 September.<sup id="cite_ref-ocafs.oca.org2_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ocafs.oca.org2-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Malankara Orthodox Church celebrates his feast on three days, 3 July (in memory of the relic translation to Edessa), 18 December (the Day he was lanced), and 21 December (when he died).<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_history_and_traditions">Later history and traditions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Later history and traditions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><i>The Passing of Mary</i>, adjudged heretical by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gelasius_I" title="Pope Gelasius I">Pope Gelasius I</a> in 494, was attributed to <a href="/wiki/Joseph_of_Arimathea" title="Joseph of Arimathea">Joseph of Arimathea</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELewis1927_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis1927-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERobinson192633_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERobinson192633-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> The document states that Thomas was the only witness of the <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Assumption of Mary</a> into heaven. The other apostles were miraculously transported to Jerusalem to witness her death. Thomas was left in India, but after her first burial, he was transported to her tomb, where he witnessed her bodily assumption into heaven, from which she dropped her <a href="/wiki/Girdle_of_Thomas" title="Girdle of Thomas">girdle</a>. In an inversion of the story of Thomas' doubts, the other apostles are skeptical of Thomas' story until they see the empty tomb and the girdle.<sup id="cite_ref-ccel_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ccel-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Thomas' receipt of the girdle is commonly depicted in medieval and pre-<a href="/wiki/Council_of_Trent" title="Council of Trent">Council of Trent</a> Renaissance art.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENorman19931–42_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENorman19931–42-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mission_in_India">Mission in India</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Mission in India">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a>, <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_India" title="Christianity in India">Christianity in India</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Kerala" title="Christianity in Kerala">Christianity in Kerala</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Postal_stamp_of_St_Thomas.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Postal_stamp_of_St_Thomas.jpg/170px-Postal_stamp_of_St_Thomas.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Postal_stamp_of_St_Thomas.jpg/255px-Postal_stamp_of_St_Thomas.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/fa/Postal_stamp_of_St_Thomas.jpg 2x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="397" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/India_Post" title="India Post">Postal Department of India</a> issued a stamp commemorating his mission to the country.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Silk_route.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Silk_route.jpg/220px-Silk_route.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Silk_route.jpg/330px-Silk_route.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Silk_route.jpg/440px-Silk_route.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2868" data-file-height="1866" /></a><figcaption>Map of ancient <a href="/wiki/Silk_Road" title="Silk Road">Silk Road</a> and <a href="/wiki/Spice_Route" class="mw-redirect" title="Spice Route">Spice Route</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>According to traditional accounts of the <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a> of India, the Apostle Thomas landed in <a href="/wiki/Muziris" title="Muziris">Muziris</a> (<a href="/wiki/Cranganore_Fort" class="mw-redirect" title="Cranganore Fort">Cranganore</a>) on the Kerala coast in AD 52 and was martyred in <a href="/wiki/Mylapore" title="Mylapore">Mylapore</a>, near <a href="/wiki/Chennai" title="Chennai">Madras</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a> in AD 72.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJohnsonZacharia2016_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJohnsonZacharia2016-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-stthoma_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stthoma-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFahlbuschBromileyLochmanMbiti2008285_1-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFahlbuschBromileyLochmanMbiti2008285-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESlapak199527_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESlapak199527-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The port was destroyed in 1341 by a massive flood that realigned the coasts. He is believed by the <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christian" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Thomas Christian">Saint Thomas Christian</a> tradition to have established <a href="/wiki/Ezharappallikal" class="mw-redirect" title="Ezharappallikal">seven churches (communities)</a> in Kerala. These churches are at Kodungallur, <a href="/wiki/St._Thomas_Syro-Malabar_Catholic_Church,_Palayoor" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Thomas Syro-Malabar Catholic Church, Palayoor">Palayoor</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kottakkavu_Mar_Thoma_Syro-Malabar_Pilgrim_Church,_North_Paravur" class="mw-redirect" title="Kottakkavu Mar Thoma Syro-Malabar Pilgrim Church, North Paravur">Kottakkavu</a> (Paravur), <a href="/wiki/Kokkamangalam" title="Kokkamangalam">Kokkamangalam</a>, <a href="/wiki/Niranam_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Niranam Church">Niranam</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nilackal_St._Thomas_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Nilackal St. Thomas Church">Nilackal (Chayal)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kollam" title="Kollam">Kollam</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Thiruvithamcode_Arappally" class="mw-redirect" title="Thiruvithamcode Arappally">Thiruvithamcode</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ppn_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ppn-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Thomas baptized several families.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMani201614_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMani201614-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Many families claim to have origins almost as far back as these, and the religious historian Robert Eric Frykenberg notes that: "Whatever dubious historicity may be attached to such local traditions, there can be little doubt as to their great antiquity or to their great appeal in the popular imagination."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFrykenberg2008101–102_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrykenberg2008101–102-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>It was to a land of dark people he was sent, to clothe them by Baptism in white robes. His grateful dawn dispelled India's painful darkness. It was his mission to espouse India to the One-Begotten. The merchant is blessed for having so great a treasure. <a href="/wiki/Edessa,_Mesopotamia" class="mw-redirect" title="Edessa, Mesopotamia">Edessa</a> thus became the blessed city by possessing the greatest pearl India could yield. Thomas works miracles in India, and at Edessa Thomas is destined to baptize peoples perverse and steeped in darkness, and that in the land of India.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite>Hymns of <a href="/wiki/Ephrem_the_Syrian" title="Ephrem the Syrian">Saint Ephrem</a>, edited by Lamy (Ephr. Hymni et Sermones, IV).</cite></div></blockquote>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><div class="poem">
<p>... Into what land shall I fly from the just?<br />
I stirred up Death the Apostles to slay, that by their death I might escape their blows.<br />
But harder still am I now stricken: the Apostle I slew in India has overtaken me in Edessa; here and there he is all himself.<br />
There went I, and there was he: here and there to my grief I find him.
</p>
</div><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite>quoted in <a href="#CITEREFMedlycott1905">Medlycott 1905</a>, Ch II</cite></div></blockquote>
<p><a href="/wiki/Ephrem_the_Syrian" title="Ephrem the Syrian">Ephrem the Syrian</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_the_Church" title="Doctor of the Church">doctor</a> of <a href="/wiki/Syriac_Christianity" title="Syriac Christianity">Syriac Christianity</a>, writes in the forty-second of his "Carmina Nisibina" that the Apostle was put to death in India, and that his remains were subsequently buried in <a href="/wiki/Edessa" title="Edessa">Edessa</a>, brought there by an unnamed merchant.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMedlycott1905157_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMedlycott1905157-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tomb_of_St._Thomas_in_India.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Tomb_of_St._Thomas_in_India.JPG/220px-Tomb_of_St._Thomas_in_India.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Tomb_of_St._Thomas_in_India.JPG/330px-Tomb_of_St._Thomas_in_India.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Tomb_of_St._Thomas_in_India.JPG/440px-Tomb_of_St._Thomas_in_India.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2394" data-file-height="1756" /></a><figcaption>The tomb of Saint Thomas the Apostle in <a href="/wiki/Mylapore" title="Mylapore">Mylapore</a>, India</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to Eusebius' record, Thomas and Bartholomew were assigned to <a href="/wiki/Parthia" title="Parthia">Parthia</a> and northwest India.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMedlycott19051–17,_213–297_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMedlycott19051–17,_213–297-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFarquhar192630_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFarquhar192630-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESmith1914235_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith1914235-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrown195649–59_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown195649–59-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> The <i><a href="/wiki/Didascalia" class="mw-redirect" title="Didascalia">Didascalia</a></i> (dating from the end of the 3rd century) states, "India and all countries condering it, even to the farthest seas... received the apostolic ordinances from Judas Thomas, who was a guide and ruler in the church which he built."
</p><p>According to traditional accounts, Thomas is believed to have left northwest India when an attack threatened and traveled by vessel to the <a href="/wiki/Malabar_Coast" title="Malabar Coast">Malabar Coast</a>, possibly visiting southeast <a href="/wiki/Arabia" class="mw-redirect" title="Arabia">Arabia</a> and Socotra en route, and landing at the former flourishing port of <a href="/wiki/Muziris" title="Muziris">Muziris</a> (modern-day <a href="/wiki/North_Paravur" title="North Paravur">North Paravur</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kodungalloor" class="mw-redirect" title="Kodungalloor">Kodungalloor</a>) (c. AD 50) in the company of a Jewish merchant Abbanes/Habban (Schonfield, 1984,125).<sup id="cite_ref-ppn_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ppn-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (June 2022)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> From there he is said to have preached the gospel throughout the Malabar coast. The various churches he founded were located mainly on the <a href="/wiki/Periyar_River" title="Periyar River">Periyar River</a> and its tributaries and along the coast, where there were Jewish colonies. In accordance with apostolic custom, Thomas ordained teachers and leaders or elders, who were reported to be the earliest ministry of the Malankara Church.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Peter_Paul_Rubens_-_Martyrdom_of_St_Thomas.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Peter_Paul_Rubens_-_Martyrdom_of_St_Thomas.jpg/170px-Peter_Paul_Rubens_-_Martyrdom_of_St_Thomas.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="248" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Peter_Paul_Rubens_-_Martyrdom_of_St_Thomas.jpg/255px-Peter_Paul_Rubens_-_Martyrdom_of_St_Thomas.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Peter_Paul_Rubens_-_Martyrdom_of_St_Thomas.jpg/340px-Peter_Paul_Rubens_-_Martyrdom_of_St_Thomas.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1351" data-file-height="1970" /></a><figcaption><i>Martyrdom of Saint Thomas</i> by <a href="/wiki/Peter_Paul_Rubens" title="Peter Paul Rubens">Peter Paul Rubens</a>, 1636–1638, in the <a href="/wiki/National_Gallery_Prague" title="National Gallery Prague">National Gallery Prague</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Chennai,_basilica_di_san_tommaso_apostolo,_museo,_reliquiario_dell%27osso_di_s._tommaso_e_della_lancia_che_lo_uccise,_02.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Chennai%2C_basilica_di_san_tommaso_apostolo%2C_museo%2C_reliquiario_dell%27osso_di_s._tommaso_e_della_lancia_che_lo_uccise%2C_02.jpg/170px-Chennai%2C_basilica_di_san_tommaso_apostolo%2C_museo%2C_reliquiario_dell%27osso_di_s._tommaso_e_della_lancia_che_lo_uccise%2C_02.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="237" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Chennai%2C_basilica_di_san_tommaso_apostolo%2C_museo%2C_reliquiario_dell%27osso_di_s._tommaso_e_della_lancia_che_lo_uccise%2C_02.jpg/255px-Chennai%2C_basilica_di_san_tommaso_apostolo%2C_museo%2C_reliquiario_dell%27osso_di_s._tommaso_e_della_lancia_che_lo_uccise%2C_02.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Chennai%2C_basilica_di_san_tommaso_apostolo%2C_museo%2C_reliquiario_dell%27osso_di_s._tommaso_e_della_lancia_che_lo_uccise%2C_02.jpg/340px-Chennai%2C_basilica_di_san_tommaso_apostolo%2C_museo%2C_reliquiario_dell%27osso_di_s._tommaso_e_della_lancia_che_lo_uccise%2C_02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3536" data-file-height="4920" /></a><figcaption>The reliquary of the spear which killed St. Thomas, in <a href="/wiki/Chennai" title="Chennai">Chennai</a>, India</figcaption></figure>
<p>According to Syrian Christian tradition, Thomas was killed with a spear at <a href="/wiki/St._Thomas_Mount" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Thomas Mount">St. Thomas Mount</a> in <a href="/wiki/Chennai" title="Chennai">Chennai</a> on 3 July in AD 72, and his body was interred in Mylapore. Latin Church tradition holds 21 December as his date of death.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFarmer2011418_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFarmer2011418-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Ephrem the Syrian states that the Apostle was killed in India, and that his relics were taken then to Edessa. This is the earliest known record of his death.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMarco_Polo1920117_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarco_Polo1920117-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The records of <a href="/wiki/Duarte_Barbosa" title="Duarte Barbosa">Barbosa</a> from the early 16th century record that the tomb was then maintained by a Muslim who kept a lamp burning there.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHunter1886237_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHunter1886237-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/St._Thomas_Cathedral_Basilica,_Chennai" title="St. Thomas Cathedral Basilica, Chennai">St. Thomas Cathedral Basilica, Chennai</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a>, India presently located at the tomb was first built in the 16th century by the Portuguese, and rebuilt in the 19th century by the British.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill200429_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeill200429-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/St._Thomas_Mount" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Thomas Mount">St. Thomas Mount</a> has been a revered site by Muslims and Christians since at least the 16th century.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHunter188631_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHunter188631-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Possible_visit_to_China">Possible visit to China</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Possible visit to China">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Thomas's alleged visit to China is mentioned in the books and church traditions of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a> in India<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBays2011Ch._1_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBays2011Ch._1-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> who, for a part, claim descent from the early Christians evangelized by Thomas the Apostle in AD 52. For example, it is found in the Malayalam ballad <i>Thomas Ramban Pattu</i> (The Song of the Lord Thomas) with the earliest manuscript being from the 17th century. The sources clearly have Thomas coming to India, then to China, and back to India, where he died.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBays2011Ch._1_58-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBays2011Ch._1-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In other attested sources, the tradition of making Thomas the apostle of China is found in the "Law of Christianity" (Fiqh al-naṣrāniyya),<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHoenerbachSpies1956_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoenerbachSpies1956-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> a compilation of juridical literature by <a href="/wiki/Ibn_al-%E1%B9%ACayyib" class="mw-redirect" title="Ibn al-Ṭayyib">Ibn al-Ṭayyib</a> (<a href="/wiki/Nestorianism" title="Nestorianism">Nestorian</a> theologian and physician who died in 1043 in <a href="/wiki/Baghdad" title="Baghdad">Baghdad</a>). Later, in the <a href="/wiki/Nomocanon" title="Nomocanon">Nomocanon</a> of <a href="/wiki/Abdisho_bar_Berika" title="Abdisho bar Berika">Abdisho bar Berika</a> (metropolitan of Nisibis and Armenia, died in 1318) and the breviary of the <a href="/wiki/Chaldean_Catholic_Church" title="Chaldean Catholic Church">Chaldean Church</a><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGilmanKlimkeit2016_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGilmanKlimkeit2016-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> it is written:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>1. Through St. Thomas the error of idolatry vanished from India.
</p><p>2. Through St. Thomas the Chinese and Ethiopians were converted to the truth.
</p><p>3. Through St. Thomas they accepted the sacrament of baptism and the adoption of sons.
</p><p>4. Through St. Thomas they believed in and confessed the Father, the Son, and Holy Spirit.
</p><p>5. Through St. Thomas they preserved the accepted faith of the one God.
</p><p>6. Through St. Thomas the life-giving splendors rose in all India.
</p><p>
7. Through St. Thomas the Kingdom of Heaven took wing and ascended to <b>China</b>.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite>Translated by <a href="/wiki/Athanasius_Kircher" title="Athanasius Kircher">Athanasius Kircher</a> in <a href="/wiki/China_Illustrata" title="China Illustrata">China Illustrata</a> (1667), <i>Office of St. Thomas for the Second Nocturn</i>, Gaza of the Church of St. Thomas of Malabar, Chaldean Breviary</cite></div></blockquote>
<p>In its nascent form, this tradition is found at the earliest in the <a href="/wiki/Zuqnin_Chronicle" title="Zuqnin Chronicle">Zuqnin Chronicle</a> (AD 775) and may have originated in the late <a href="/wiki/Sasanian_Empire" title="Sasanian Empire">Sasanian</a> period.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETubach1995397–430_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETubach1995397–430-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETakahashi2011_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETakahashi2011-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Perhaps it originated as a 3rd-century pseudepigraphon where Thomas would have converted the Magi (in the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_Matthew" title="Gospel of Matthew">Gospel of Matthew</a>) to Christianity as they dwelled in the land of Shir (land of <a href="/wiki/Serica" title="Serica">Seres</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tarim_Basin" title="Tarim Basin">Tarim Basin</a>, near what was the world's easternmost sea for many people in antiquity).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAndrade201858–59_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAndrade201858–59-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, the testimony of <a href="/wiki/Arnobius" title="Arnobius">Arnobius of Sicca</a>, active shortly after AD 300, maintains that the Christian message had arrived in India and among the Persians, Medians, and Parthians (along with the <a href="/wiki/Serica" title="Serica">Seres</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEArnobius_of_Sicca1949125_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEArnobius_of_Sicca1949125-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Possible_travel_into_Indonesia">Possible travel into Indonesia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Possible travel into Indonesia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Kurt_E._Koch" title="Kurt E. Koch">Kurt E. Koch</a>, Thomas the Apostle possibly traveled into Indonesia via India with Indian traders.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKoch1972_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKoch1972-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Paraguayan_legend">Paraguayan legend</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Paraguayan legend">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Ancient oral tradition retained by the <a href="/wiki/Guaran%C3%AD_people" title="Guaraní people">Guaraní</a> tribes of <a href="/wiki/Paraguay" title="Paraguay">Paraguay</a> claims that the Apostle Thomas was in Paraguay and preached to them.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>in the estate of our college, called Paraguay, and twenty leagues distant from Asumpcion. This place stretches out on one side into a pleasant plain, affording pasture to a vast quantity of cattle; on the other, where it looks towards the south, it is surrounded by hills and rocks; in one of which a cross piled up of three large stones is visited, and held in great veneration by the natives for the sake of St. Thomas; for they believe, and firmly maintain, that the Apostle, seated on these stones as on a chair, formerly preached to the assembled Indians.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><a href="#CITEREFDobrizhoffer1822">Dobrizhoffer 1822</a>, p.&#160;385</cite></div></blockquote>
<p>Almost 150 years prior to Dobrizhoffer's arrival in Paraguay, another Jesuit Missionary, F. J. <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Ruiz_de_Montoya" title="Antonio Ruiz de Montoya">Antonio Ruiz de Montoya</a> recollected the same oral traditions from the Paraguayan tribes. He wrote:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>...The paraguayan tribes they have this very curious tradition. They claim that a very holy man (Thomas the Apostle himself), whom they call "Paí Thome", lived amongst them and preached to them the Holy Truth, wandering and carrying a wooden cross on his back.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><a href="#CITEREFRuiz_de_Montoya1639">Ruiz de Montoya 1639</a>, Ch XVIII</cite></div></blockquote>
<p>The sole recorded research done about the subject was during <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Gaspar_Rodr%C3%ADguez_de_Francia" title="José Gaspar Rodríguez de Francia">José Gaspar Rodríguez de Francia</a>'s reign after the <a href="/wiki/Independence_of_Paraguay" title="Independence of Paraguay">Independence of Paraguay</a>. This is mentioned by Franz Wisner von Morgenstern, an Austro-Hungarian engineer who served in the Paraguayan armies prior and during the <a href="/wiki/Paraguayan_War" title="Paraguayan War">Paraguayan War</a>. According to Von Morgenstern, some Paraguayan miners while working nearby some hills at the <a href="/wiki/Caaguaz%C3%BA_Department" title="Caaguazú Department">Caaguazú Department</a> found some stones with ancient letters carved in them. <a href="/wiki/Dictator" title="Dictator">Dictator</a> Francia sent his finest experts to inspect those stones, and they concluded that the letters carved in those stones were <a href="/wiki/Hebrew_language" title="Hebrew language">Hebrew</a>-like symbols, but they couldn't translate them nor figure out the exact date when those letters were carved.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEvon_Morgenstern1998198_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEvon_Morgenstern1998198-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> No further recorded investigations exists, and according to Wisner, people believed that the letters were made by Thomas the Apostle, following the tradition.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relics">Relics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Relics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:ShrineOfSaintThomasAtMeliapore18thCentury.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/ShrineOfSaintThomasAtMeliapore18thCentury.jpg/170px-ShrineOfSaintThomasAtMeliapore18thCentury.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="214" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/ShrineOfSaintThomasAtMeliapore18thCentury.jpg/255px-ShrineOfSaintThomasAtMeliapore18thCentury.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/ShrineOfSaintThomasAtMeliapore18thCentury.jpg/340px-ShrineOfSaintThomasAtMeliapore18thCentury.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1126" data-file-height="1415" /></a><figcaption>Shrine of Saint Thomas in <a href="/wiki/Mylapore" title="Mylapore">Mylapore</a>, 18th-century print</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ortona_-Reliquary_chest_of_Saint_Thomas-_2006_by-RaBoe_02.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Ortona_-Reliquary_chest_of_Saint_Thomas-_2006_by-RaBoe_02.jpg/220px-Ortona_-Reliquary_chest_of_Saint_Thomas-_2006_by-RaBoe_02.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Ortona_-Reliquary_chest_of_Saint_Thomas-_2006_by-RaBoe_02.jpg/330px-Ortona_-Reliquary_chest_of_Saint_Thomas-_2006_by-RaBoe_02.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Ortona_-Reliquary_chest_of_Saint_Thomas-_2006_by-RaBoe_02.jpg/440px-Ortona_-Reliquary_chest_of_Saint_Thomas-_2006_by-RaBoe_02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1134" data-file-height="877" /></a><figcaption>Relics of Thomas in the Cathedral of <a href="/wiki/Ortona" title="Ortona">Ortona</a></figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Mylapore">Mylapore</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Mylapore">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Traditional accounts say that the Apostle Thomas preached not only in Kerala but also in other parts of Southern India – and a few relics are still kept at <a href="/wiki/San_Thome_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="San Thome Basilica">San Thome Basilica</a> in Mylapore neighborhood in the central part of the city of <a href="/wiki/Chennai" title="Chennai">Chennai</a> in India.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMuthiah2014_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMuthiah2014-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Marco_Polo" title="Marco Polo">Marco Polo</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Venetian</a> traveller and author of <i>Description of the World,</i> popularly known as <i>Il Milione,</i> is reputed to have visited Southern India in 1288 and 1292. The first date has been rejected as he was in China at the time, but the second date is generally accepted.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMuthiah2014_67-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMuthiah2014-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Edessa">Edessa</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Edessa">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>According to tradition, in AD 232, the greater portion of relics of the Apostle Thomas are said to have been sent by an Indian king and brought from Mylapore to the city of <a href="/wiki/Edessa,_Mesopotamia" class="mw-redirect" title="Edessa, Mesopotamia">Edessa, Mesopotamia</a>, on which occasion his Syriac <i>Acts</i> were written.
</p><p>The Indian king is named as "Mazdai" in Syriac sources, "Misdeos" and "Misdeus" in Greek and Latin sources respectively, which has been connected to the "Bazdeo" on the Kushan coinage of <a href="/wiki/Vasudeva_I" title="Vasudeva I">Vasudeva I</a>, the transition between "M" and "B" being a current one in Classical sources for Indian names.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBussagli1965255_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBussagli1965255-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> The martyrologist <a href="/wiki/Martyrology_of_Rabban_Sliba" title="Martyrology of Rabban Sliba">Rabban Sliba</a> dedicated a special day to both the Indian king, his family, and Saint Thomas:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p><i>Coronatio Thomae apostoli et Misdeus rex Indiae, Johannes eus filius huisque mater Tertia</i> (Coronation of Thomas the Apostle, and Misdeus king of India, together with his son Johannes (thought to be a latinization of <i>Vizan</i>) and his mother Tertia) Rabban Sliba</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><a href="#CITEREFBussagli1965">Bussagli 1965</a>, p.&#160;255</cite></div></blockquote>
<p>In the 4th century, the martyrium erected over his burial place brought pilgrims to Edessa. In the 380s, <a href="/wiki/Egeria_(pilgrim)" title="Egeria (pilgrim)">Egeria</a> described her visit in a letter she sent to her community of nuns at home (<i>Itineraria Egeriae</i>):<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<blockquote><p>We arrived at Edessa in the Name of Christ our God, and, on our arrival, we straightway repaired to the church and memorial of saint Thomas. There, according to custom, prayers were made and the other things that were customary in the holy places were done; we read also some things concerning saint Thomas himself. The church there is very great, very beautiful and of new construction, well worthy to be the house of God, and as there was much that I desired to see, it was necessary for me to make a three days' stay there.</p></blockquote>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Theodoret" title="Theodoret">Theodoret of Cyrrhus</a>, the bones of Saint Thomas were transferred by <a href="/wiki/Cyrus_I_of_Edessa" title="Cyrus I of Edessa">Cyrus I</a>, Bishop of Edessa, from the martyrium outside of Edessa to a church in the south-west corner of the city on 22 August 394.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHarvey2005124_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarvey2005124-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 441, the <i><a href="/wiki/Magister_militum_per_Orientem" class="mw-redirect" title="Magister militum per Orientem">Magister militum per Orientem</a></i> <a href="/wiki/Anatolius_(consul_440)" class="mw-redirect" title="Anatolius (consul 440)">Anatolius</a> donated a silver coffin to hold the relics.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESegal2005174–176,_250_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESegal2005174–176,_250-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In AD 522, <a href="/wiki/Cosmas_Indicopleustes" title="Cosmas Indicopleustes">Cosmas Indicopleustes</a> (called the Alexandrian) visited the Malabar Coast. He is the first traveller who mentions Syrian Christians in Malabar, in his book <i>Christian Topography.</i> He mentions that in the town of "Kalliana" (Quilon or Kollam) there was a bishop who had been consecrated in Persia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESadasivan2000410_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESadasivan2000410-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1144, the city was conquered by the <a href="/wiki/Zengid_dynasty" title="Zengid dynasty">Zengids</a> and the shrine destroyed.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESegal2005174–176,_250_71-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESegal2005174–176,_250-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Chios_and_Ortona">Chios and Ortona</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Chios and Ortona">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cattedrale-di-San-Tommaso-al-crepuscolo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Cattedrale-di-San-Tommaso-al-crepuscolo.jpg/220px-Cattedrale-di-San-Tommaso-al-crepuscolo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="164" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Cattedrale-di-San-Tommaso-al-crepuscolo.jpg/330px-Cattedrale-di-San-Tommaso-al-crepuscolo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Cattedrale-di-San-Tommaso-al-crepuscolo.jpg/440px-Cattedrale-di-San-Tommaso-al-crepuscolo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="2984" /></a><figcaption>Ortona's Basilica of Saint Thomas</figcaption></figure>
<p>The reputed relics of Saint Thomas remained at Edessa until they were moved to <a href="/wiki/Chios" title="Chios">Chios</a> in 1258.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThurston1913_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThurston1913-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> Some portion of the relics were later transferred again, and now rest in the Cathedral of Saint Thomas the Apostle in <a href="/wiki/Ortona" title="Ortona">Ortona</a>, Italy. However, the skull of Thomas is said to be at <a href="/wiki/Monastery_of_Saint_John_the_Theologian" title="Monastery of Saint John the Theologian">Monastery of Saint John the Theologian</a> on the Greek island of <a href="/wiki/Patmos" title="Patmos">Patmos</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESanidopoulos2010_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESanidopoulos2010-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ortona's three galleys reached the island of Chios in 1258, led by General Leone Acciaiuoli. Chios was considered the island where Thomas, after his death in India, had been buried. A portion fought around the Peloponnese and the Aegean islands, the other in the sea lapping at the then Syrian coast. The three galleys of Ortona moved on the second front of the war and reached the island of Chios.
</p><p>The tale is provided by Giambattista De Lectis, physician and writer of the 16th century of Ortona. After the looting, the navarca Ortona Leone went to pray in the main church of the island of Chios and was drawn to a chapel adorned and resplendent with lights. An elderly priest, through an interpreter informed him that in that oratory was venerated the Body of Saint Thomas the Apostle. Leone, filled with an unusual sweetness, gathered in deep prayer. At that moment a light hand twice invited him to come closer. The navarca Leone reached out and took a bone from the largest hole of the tombstone, on which were carved the Greek letters and a halo depicted a bishop from the waist up. He was the confirmation of what he had said the old priest and that you are indeed in the presence of the Apostle's body. He went back on the galley and planned the theft for the next night, along with fellow Ruggiero Grogno. They lifted the heavy gravestone and watched the underlying relics. The wrapped in snow-white cloths them laid in a wooden box (stored at Ortona to the looting of 1566) and brought them aboard the galley. Leone, then, along with other comrades, he returned again in the church, took the tombstone and took her away. Just the Chinardo admiral was aware of the precious cargo moved all the sailors of the Muslim faith on other ships and ordered him to take the route to Ortona.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ortona_-San_Tommaso_-_Portal-_2006_by-RaBoe_01.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Ortona_-San_Tommaso_-_Portal-_2006_by-RaBoe_01.jpg/170px-Ortona_-San_Tommaso_-_Portal-_2006_by-RaBoe_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Ortona_-San_Tommaso_-_Portal-_2006_by-RaBoe_01.jpg/255px-Ortona_-San_Tommaso_-_Portal-_2006_by-RaBoe_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Ortona_-San_Tommaso_-_Portal-_2006_by-RaBoe_01.jpg/340px-Ortona_-San_Tommaso_-_Portal-_2006_by-RaBoe_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="851" data-file-height="1134" /></a><figcaption>Portal of Ortona, Saint Thomas' Basilica</figcaption></figure>
<p>He landed at the port of Ortona 6 September 1258. According to the story of De Lectis, he was informed the abbot Jacopo was responsible for Ortona Church, which predisposed full provision for hospitality felt and shared by all the people. Since then the body of the apostle and the gravestone are preserved in the crypt of the Basilica. In 1259 a parchment written in Bari by the court under John Peacock contracts, the presence of five witnesses, preserved in Ortona at the Diocesan Library, confirming the veracity of that event, reported, as mentioned, by Giambattista De Lectis, physician and writer Ortona of the 16th century.
</p><p>The relics resisted both the Saracen looting of 1566, and the destruction of the Nazis in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Ortona" title="Battle of Ortona">battle of Ortona</a> fought in late December 1943. The basilica was blown up because the belfry was considered a lookout point by the allies, coming by sea from San Vito Chietino. The relics, together with the treasure of Saint Thomas, were intended by the Germans to be sold, but the monks entombed them inside the bell tower, the only surviving part of the semi-ruined church.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ortona_-San_Tommaso-_2006_by-RaBoe_03.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Ortona_-San_Tommaso-_2006_by-RaBoe_03.jpg/220px-Ortona_-San_Tommaso-_2006_by-RaBoe_03.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Ortona_-San_Tommaso-_2006_by-RaBoe_03.jpg/330px-Ortona_-San_Tommaso-_2006_by-RaBoe_03.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Ortona_-San_Tommaso-_2006_by-RaBoe_03.jpg/440px-Ortona_-San_Tommaso-_2006_by-RaBoe_03.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1134" data-file-height="851" /></a><figcaption>Slab of chalcedony which covered the Apostle's relics at Chios, now in the Basilica of Ortona<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMedlycott1905_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMedlycott1905-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>The tombstone of Thomas, brought to Ortona from Chios along with the relics of the Apostle, are preserved in the crypt of St Thomas Basilica, behind the altar. The urn containing the bones is placed under the altar. It is the cover of a fake coffin, fairly widespread burial form in the early Christian world, as the top of a tomb of less expensive material. The plaque has an inscription and a bas-relief that refer, in many respects, to the Syro-Mesopotamian. Tombstone Thomas the Apostle on inclusion can be read, in Greek characters uncial, the expression 'osios thomas, that Saint Thomas. It can be dated from the point of view palaeographic and lexical to the 3rd–5th century, a time when the term osios is still used as a synonym of aghios in that holy is he that is in the grace of God and is inserted in the church: the two vocabulary, therefore, indicate the Christians. In the particular case of Saint Thomas' plaque, the word osios can be the translation of the word Syriac <i>mar</i> (Lord), attributed in the ancient world, but also to the present day, is a saint to be a bishop.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Iraq">Iraq</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Iraq">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The finger bones of Saint Thomas were discovered during restoration work at the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Saint_Thomas,_Mosul" title="Church of Saint Thomas, Mosul">Church of Saint Thomas</a> in <a href="/wiki/Mosul" title="Mosul">Mosul</a>, <a href="/wiki/Iraq" title="Iraq">Iraq</a> in 1964,<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> and were housed there until the <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_Mosul" title="Fall of Mosul">Fall of Mosul</a>, after which the relics were transferred to the <a href="/wiki/Mar_Mattai_Monastery" class="mw-redirect" title="Mar Mattai Monastery">Monastery of Saint Matthew</a> on 17 June 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEArraf2018_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEArraf2018-76">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-theorthodoxchurch.info_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-theorthodoxchurch.info-77">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Succession">Succession</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Succession">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>As per the tradition of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a>, St. Thomas the Apostle established his throne in India and ordained <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mar_Keppa&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mar Keppa (page does not exist)">Mar Keppa</a>, a Chera prince, as his successor.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_of_St._Thomas_the_Apostle">See of St. Thomas the Apostle</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: See of St. Thomas the Apostle">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>As per the tradition of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a>, Saint Thomas the Apostle established his throne in India and <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a> was his See (Kolla Hendo), therefore the see of the metropolitan of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a> was <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a> and used the title Metropolitan and Gate of all India.<sup id="cite_ref-Theo_of_CoE_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Theo_of_CoE-79">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> In <a href="/wiki/Syriac_language" title="Syriac language">Syriac</a> Manuscript Vatican Syriac Codex 22 the title given for the Metropolitan of the <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas_Christians" title="Saint Thomas Christians">Saint Thomas Christians</a> was "the superintendent and ruler of the holy see of St. Thomas the Apostle".
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Historical_references">Historical references</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Historical references">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:By_the_command_of_an_Indian_King_he_was_thrust_through_with_Lances.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/By_the_command_of_an_Indian_King_he_was_thrust_through_with_Lances.jpg/170px-By_the_command_of_an_Indian_King_he_was_thrust_through_with_Lances.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="219" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/By_the_command_of_an_Indian_King_he_was_thrust_through_with_Lances.jpg/255px-By_the_command_of_an_Indian_King_he_was_thrust_through_with_Lances.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/By_the_command_of_an_Indian_King_he_was_thrust_through_with_Lances.jpg/340px-By_the_command_of_an_Indian_King_he_was_thrust_through_with_Lances.jpg 2x" data-file-width="658" data-file-height="849" /></a><figcaption>"By the command of an Indian King he was thrust through with lances", 1739 engraving</figcaption></figure>
<p>A number of early Christian writings written during the centuries immediately following the first Ecumenical Council of 325 mention Thomas' mission.
</p><p>The <i>Transitus Mariae</i> describes each of the apostles purportedly being temporarily transported to heaven during the <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Assumption of Mary</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Acts_of_Thomas">Acts of Thomas</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Acts of Thomas">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The main source is the <a href="/wiki/New_Testament_apocrypha" title="New Testament apocrypha">apocryphal</a> <a href="/wiki/Acts_of_Thomas" title="Acts of Thomas">Acts of Thomas</a>, sometimes called by its full name <i>The Acts of Judas Thomas</i>, written circa 180–230 AD.<sup id="cite_ref-Apocryphal_Acts_of_the_Apostles_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Apocryphal_Acts_of_the_Apostles-80">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-constom_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constom-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> These are generally regarded by various Christian religions as <a href="/wiki/Apocryphal" class="mw-redirect" title="Apocryphal">apocryphal</a>, or even heretical. The two centuries that lapsed between the life of the apostle and the recording of this work cast doubt on their authenticity.
</p><p>The king, Misdeus (or Mizdeos), was infuriated when Thomas converted the queen Tertia, the king's son Juzanes, sister-in-law princess Mygdonia and her friend Markia. Misdeus led Thomas outside the city and ordered four soldiers to take him to the nearby hill, where the soldiers speared Thomas, killing him. After Thomas' death, Syphorus was elected the first <a href="/wiki/Presbyter" title="Presbyter">presbyter</a> of Mazdai by the surviving converts, while Juzanes was the first <a href="/wiki/Deacon" title="Deacon">deacon</a>. (The names Misdeus, Tertia, Juzanes, Syphorus, Markia and Mygdonia (c.f. <a href="/wiki/Mygdonia" title="Mygdonia">Mygdonia</a>, a province of <a href="/wiki/Mesopotamia" title="Mesopotamia">Mesopotamia</a>) may suggest Greek descent or cultural influences.<sup id="cite_ref-constom_81-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constom-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Greek traders had long visited Muziris. Greek kingdoms in northern India and Bactria, founded by <a href="/wiki/Alexander_the_Great" title="Alexander the Great">Alexander the Great</a>, were vassals<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement" title="Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute"><span title="The material near this tag is possibly inaccurate or nonfactual. (July 2019)">dubious</span></a>&#32;<span class="metadata"> &#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:Thomas_the_Apostle#Dubious" title="Talk:Thomas the Apostle">discuss</a></span></i>&#93;</sup> of the Indo-Parthians.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMookerji196628_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMookerji196628-82">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Incredulity_of_Saint_Thomas_by_Caravaggio.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/The_Incredulity_of_Saint_Thomas_by_Caravaggio.jpg/220px-The_Incredulity_of_Saint_Thomas_by_Caravaggio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="160" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/The_Incredulity_of_Saint_Thomas_by_Caravaggio.jpg/330px-The_Incredulity_of_Saint_Thomas_by_Caravaggio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/The_Incredulity_of_Saint_Thomas_by_Caravaggio.jpg/440px-The_Incredulity_of_Saint_Thomas_by_Caravaggio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1070" data-file-height="776" /></a><figcaption><i>The Incredulity of Saint Thomas</i> by <a href="/wiki/Caravaggio" title="Caravaggio">Caravaggio</a></figcaption></figure>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Doctrine_of_the_Apostles">Doctrine of the Apostles</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Doctrine of the Apostles">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The Doctrine of the Apostles as reflected in <a href="#CITEREFCureton1864">Cureton 1864</a>, pp.&#160;32–34 attests that Thomas had written Christian doctrine from India.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>India and all its own countries, and those bordering on it, even to the farther sea, received the Apostle's hand of Priesthood from Judas Thomas, who was Guide and Ruler in the Church which he built and ministered there". In what follows "the whole Persia of the Assyrians and Medes, and of the countries round about Babylon… even to the borders of the Indians and even to the country of <a href="/wiki/Gog_and_Magog" title="Gog and Magog">Gog and Magog</a>" are said to have received the Apostles' Hand of Priesthood from <a href="/wiki/Aggai_(bishop)" title="Aggai (bishop)">Aggaeus</a> the disciple of <a href="/wiki/Addai_of_Edessa" title="Addai of Edessa">Addaeus</a></p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><a href="#CITEREFCureton1864">Cureton 1864</a>, p.&#160;33</cite></div></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Origen">Origen</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Origen">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Christian philosopher <a href="/wiki/Origen" title="Origen">Origen</a> taught with great acclaim in Alexandria and then in Caesarea.<sup id="cite_ref-EcclHist_III.1s2_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EcclHist_III.1s2-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> He is the first known writer to record the <a href="/wiki/Cleromancy" title="Cleromancy">casting of lots</a> by the Apostles. <a href="/wiki/Origen" title="Origen">Origen</a>'s original work has been lost, but his statement about Parthia falling to Thomas has been preserved by <a href="/wiki/Eusebius" title="Eusebius">Eusebius</a>. "Origen, in the third chapter of his Commentary on Genesis, says that, according to tradition, Thomas's allotted field of labour was Parthia".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPerumalil197150–51_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerumalil197150–51-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-HambyeComes_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HambyeComes-85">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFarquhar192630–31_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFarquhar192630–31-86">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Eusebius">Eusebius</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Eusebius">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Quoting <a href="/wiki/Origen" title="Origen">Origen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eusebius_of_Caesarea" class="mw-redirect" title="Eusebius of Caesarea">Eusebius of Caesarea</a> says: "When the holy Apostles and disciples of our Saviour were scattered over all the world, Thomas, so the tradition has it, obtained as his portion Parthia…"<sup id="cite_ref-Panjikaran_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Panjikaran-87">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> "Judas, who is also called Thomas" has a role in the legend of king <a href="/wiki/Abgar" class="mw-redirect" title="Abgar">Abgar</a> of <a href="/wiki/Edessa,_Mesopotamia" class="mw-redirect" title="Edessa, Mesopotamia">Edessa</a> (Urfa), for having sent <a href="/wiki/Thaddeus_of_Edessa" class="mw-redirect" title="Thaddeus of Edessa">Thaddaeus</a> to preach in Edessa after the Ascension.<sup id="cite_ref-EcclHist_I.13_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EcclHist_I.13-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-EcclHist_III.1_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EcclHist_III.1-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Ephrem_the_Syrian" title="Ephrem the Syrian">Ephrem the Syrian</a> also recounts this legend.<sup id="cite_ref-Patrologia_Graeca_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patrologia_Graeca-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ephrem_the_Syrian">Ephrem the Syrian</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Ephrem the Syrian">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Many devotional hymns composed by <a href="/wiki/Ephrem_the_Syrian" title="Ephrem the Syrian">Ephrem the Syrian</a> bear witness to the <a href="/wiki/Edessa,_Mesopotamia" class="mw-redirect" title="Edessa, Mesopotamia">Edessan</a> Church's strong conviction concerning Thomas's Indian Apostolate. There the devil speaks of Thomas as "the Apostle I slew in India". Also, "The merchant brought the bones" to Edessa.<sup id="cite_ref-bickell_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bickell-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Another hymn eulogizing Saint Thomas reads "The bones the merchant hath brought". "In his several journeyings to India/ And thence on his return/ All riches/ which there he found/ Dirt in his eyes he did repute when to thy sacred bones compared". In yet another hymn Ephrem speaks of the mission of Thomas: "The earth darkened with sacrifices' fumes to illuminate", "a land of people dark fell to thy lot", "a tainted land Thomas has purified"; "India's dark night" was "flooded with light" by Thomas.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><a href="#CITEREFMedlycott1905">Medlycott 1905</a>, pp.&#160;21–32<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement" title="Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute"><span title="The material near this tag is possibly inaccurate or nonfactual. (July 2019)">dubious</span></a>&#32;<span class="metadata"> &#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:Thomas_the_Apostle#Dubious" title="Talk:Thomas the Apostle">discuss</a></span></i>&#93;</sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (July 2019)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></cite></div></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Gregory_of_Nazianzus">Gregory of Nazianzus</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Thomas_the_Apostle&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Gregory of Nazianzus">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Thomas_the_Apostle_mosaic.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Thomas_the_Apostle_mosaic.jpg/220px-Thomas_the_Apostle_mosaic.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="215" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/cf/Thomas_the_Apostle_mosaic.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="250" data-file-height="244" /></a><figcaption>Ancient mosaic of the Apostle Thomas</figcaption></figure>
